### Page 25

Bank Sector Outlook 2027 October 2026

Stablecoins are increasingly moving into the mainstream financial system as banks assess both the risks and 
opportunities they present

Stablecoins’ market capitalisation has grown significantly over the past two years. This 
growth has drawn widespread scrutiny, including from regulators, as it raises concerns 
about financial stability. Banks have also paid closer attention to the sector as it could 
represent both new risks and opportunities. 
In this piece, we review the potential impact stablecoins could have on the European 
banking sector. This is especially relevant as multiple regulatory changes are expected in 
2027. The EU is reviewing its Markets in Crypto-Assets Regulation (MiCAR), which could 
affect the estimated impact of stablecoins on the banking sector. Across the Atlantic, 
the regulatory landscape could also change next year. Discussions could result in the US 
allowing stablecoin issuers to directly distribute rewards, which would be a first. 
Overall, while the future of stablecoins is still uncertain, they promise to remain in the 
spotlight in 2027. We show that despite multiple risks identified, European banks are 
unlikely to surrender ground to fintech stablecoin issuers without a fight. 
What are stablecoins? 
Stablecoins are crypto assets issued by private institutions that promise a stable 
nominal value in a given currency, commodity or pool of assets. Stablecoins are 
therefore not issued (or backed) by a government or central authority, but rather by a 
private institution and secured by segregated reserves. 
The crypto asset is issued on distributed ledger technology (DLT), such as a blockchain, 
with three existing types of pegging mechanisms. For the sake of this analysis, we will 
focus on the main one: fiat-collateralised stablecoins. These crypto assets are backed 
1:1 by reserves of cash or liquid assets. 
In our overview publication on stablecoins (read more here), we review the sector’s 
recent development. We show that the total market capitalisation of stablecoins is

Marine Leleux 
Sector Strategist, Financials 
marine.leleux2@ing.com

Stablecoins’ growth – opportunity or 
threat for banks? 
While stablecoin adoption could significantly affect European banks, including by shifting 
their funding base, the sector could also benefit from the technology by issuing its own 
tokens. Upcoming regulatory changes could alter the impact of stablecoins on banks, but 
we should not expect banks to surrender ground to fintech issuers without a fight

### Page 26

Bank Sector Outlook 2027 October 2026

surpassing $300bn globally, 99% of which consists of USD-pegged tokens. While still 
dwarfed by, for example, bank deposits, the rapid growth of the sector over the past five 
years (up by US$250bn) has drawn a lot of attention. 
Selected stablecoins' market capitalisation over time & share of all cryptocurrencies

USDT = Tether, USDC = Circle, USDS = Sky Protocol  
Source: ING Research, IMF

Growing interest in stablecoins stems both from possible efficiency gains and the 
potential risks they pose. For stablecoins to reach the ‘broad adoption’ level of 
development, usage should extend beyond the niche crypto landscape and into the 
payments world. Several variables, including regulation, integration into payment 
systems and relative attractiveness compared with other payment systems, must be 
considered when determining the future growth of stablecoins. Read more on that in 
our piece Stablecoins 101: Uncertain growth but the stakes are high. 
Despite their uncertain future, we believe it's important to consider a scenario in which 
stablecoins become broadly adopted and systemically important as a means of 
payment. We identify multiple potential impacts of the crypto asset, including 
implications for emerging markets, interest rates, and monetary policy. In this piece, we 
will focus specifically on potential implications for the banking sector. 
Stablecoins impact on banks: Threat, opportunity, or both? 
In this section, we explore the potential implications of a broad use of stablecoins 
beyond the crypto landscape and into payment systems for banks. Despite US dollar 
stablecoins currently dominating the market, our main assumption is the widespread 
adoption of euro-denominated stablecoins in Europe. 
While stablecoins are often presented as a challenge for the banking sector, we review 
here five potential implications, not all of which are negative for financial institutions. 
A change in banks' funding 
To discuss the consequences of an uptake in stablecoin adoption on banks’ funding, two 
variables must be considered: 
1. Which assets are used to buy stablecoins 
2. What type of reserves are required by the regulation 
If most users acquire stablecoins by redeeming liquid investments, the impact for 
financial institutions is expected to remain limited. However, if EU citizens and 
corporates adopt EUR-denominated stablecoins using bank deposits, the impact on 
financial institutions could be substantially more significant. In this scenario, banks could 
face a net drop in their retail and corporate deposits and/or a shift in the composition of 
their funding base. 
1

### Page 27

Bank Sector Outlook 2027 October 2026

Importantly, the nature of banks’ funding change depends on the second key variable: 
the regulation in place, more specifically the reserve regime. Indeed, widespread 
adoption of the crypto asset with deposits could result in a drop in banks’ funding. 
However, under the EU’s MiCAR, stablecoin issuers are required to hold part of their 
reserves as deposits with European commercial banks. 
This implies that part of the amount flowing out of banks would ultimately come back to 
the sector as reserves from stablecoin issuers. Stablecoin adoption would therefore not 
translate into a net drop in funding for banks but rather in a shift in their funding base, 
where retail and corporate deposits would come back in wholesale form (more 
specifically as financial institution deposits). 
Illustration of banks' funding shift through stablecoins adoption using retail deposits*

*stylized example using MiCAR for systemic issuers 
Source: ING

This shift from retail to wholesale deposits is not without consequences. Wholesale 
deposits are known to be more volatile than retail ones and therefore represent a less 
stable funding source for banks. Additionally, capital requirements are higher for 
wholesale deposits, with, for example, higher run-off rates in the Liquidity Coverage 
Ratio (LCR) and lower available stable funding weights in the Net Stable Funding Ratio 
(NSFR). This reflects their greater volatility and related risks, making them more 
expensive for banks to hold. 
Greater reliance on wholesale deposits could also create risk management challenges 
with more complex asset-liability matching. The ease with which deposits can be 
switched into stablecoins could increase financial institutions’ exposure to economic 
cycles. 
To date, all main regulations in place ban stablecoin issuers from directly remunerating 
holders. Therefore, an environment in which interest rates are lowered (or even 
approach zero) would make holding stablecoins more attractive for users, as the 
difference with holding a deposit account is reduced. This could trigger sudden 
stablecoin adoption and a quick shift in banks’ funding. 
The opposite scenario could also pose challenges for banks. As interest rates increase, 
the attractiveness of holding stablecoins diminishes. This could lead to sudden 
redemption requests from stablecoin holders, forcing issuers to liquidate reserves held at 
banks. As a result, financial institutions could face an outflow of stablecoin issuers’ 
deposits with a simultaneous inflow of retail deposits, adding volatility to their funding 
profiles. 
In either case, these funding changes and substitution dynamics could make banks’ 
funding management more complex, costly and difficult to predict. However, as long

### Page 28

Bank Sector Outlook 2027 October 2026

as regulation prevents stablecoin issuers from directly remunerating holders, the effect 
of economic cycles is expected to remain muted, as stablecoins only become attractive 
as a deposit-like instrument in the case of a very low interest rate environment. 
Additionally, the impact is likely to vary across the banking sector. Financial institutions 
with a large base of digitally active customers could face heightened pressure from 
widespread stablecoin adoption, as the barriers to switching from deposits to crypto 
assets are lower. Differences could also emerge between deposit categories. The impact 
is expected to be especially noticeable for accounts used as payment instruments, held 
by younger, digitally native customers who tend to be less relationship-driven or loyal to 
a specific bank and generally more willing to adopt new financial technologies. 
While these points explore a scenario in which EUR-denominated stablecoins are 
widespread, it’s important to note that the impact would be different if USD-
denominated stablecoins continue to dominate the market. In that case, a significant 
uptake would not translate into a funding shift for European banks but rather into a net 
outflow of deposits. 
Indeed, these funds could flow to US stablecoin issuers, which would place the safe 
assets as required under US regulation. In other words, this would translate into a net 
outflow of European deposits towards the US economy. Aside from the monetary policy 
impact, European banks would be required to turn to alternative and more expensive 
sources of funding to compensate for the outflow of retail deposits. 
However, EU banks can also play a central role in the issuance and adoption of EUR-
denominated stablecoins to counter that effect, as we discuss below. 
Pressure on credit provision 
In addition to changing banks’ funding base, broad stablecoin adoption could also 
negatively impact credit provision through several channels. 
In a scenario where banks see a funding shift towards greater reliance on wholesale 
deposits, higher liquidity requirements may push them to take more conservative risk 
decisions. Research has shown that this could translate into banks steering their balance 
sheets away from illiquid and long-maturity loans to ensure they comply with the 
regulation, as well as reducing loan volumes, shortening maturities and increasing loan 
pricing. Overall, the European Central Bank has shown that deposit substitution could 
lead to a contraction of about 0.2% in lending to firms. 
In addition to regulatory restrictions, wholesale deposits show different volatility 
patterns from those of retail deposits, mainly by being more reactive to market and 
macroeconomic changes. Greater reliance on such deposits would, once again, imply 
more complex risk management, pushing banks to hold additional liquidity buffers 
which could increase loan pricing. 
Here, again, the impact could differ depending on the financial institution. European 
banks already showing high loan-to-deposit ratios, as well as those close to regulatory 
thresholds, could be more inclined to pass higher funding costs on through lending 
conditions in order to remain within regulatory thresholds. 
Concentration and spillover risks 
A growing discussion among regulators centres on concentration risks. As stablecoin 
issuers are required to hold part of their reserves as deposits with commercial banks, 
financial institutions could increasingly rely on their deposits for funding. As the 
proportion of banks’ funding coming from a small number of stablecoin issuers 
increases, so does concentration risk. This impact may become substantial if stablecoin 
adoption is both significant and concentrated among a handful of issuers. 
2 
3

### Page 29

Bank Sector Outlook 2027 October 2026

Concentration could also arise if stablecoin issuers maintain relationships with only a 
limited number of banks, those with both the technical capabilities and willingness to 
engage with the sector. This could create pockets of concentration on both sides of the 
business, introducing new systemic risks for the banking sector. 
Regulators across the globe have been increasingly attentive to the development of 
stablecoins. However, as concentration risks between banks and stablecoin issuers 
potentially emerge, they might also see the need to update their frameworks to ensure 
that such risks are correctly accounted for and captured in stress testing. 
In addition, the current EU regulation also creates a direct link between the banking 
sector and crypto asset issuers, increasing spillover risks. In the event of a run on 
stablecoins, where the crypto asset suddenly loses value and users redeem their 
holdings, banks carrying the issuer’s reserves could face sudden and significant 
redemption requests. This could transmit a shock in the crypto sector to the broader 
financial system. The reverse is also true, with stablecoin issuers potentially affected by 
the failure of, or reputational damage to, the commercial bank they hold reserves with. 
These concerns promise to remain relevant in 2027 as the EU is reviewing its MiCA 
Regulation. In May 2026, the European Commission opened a consultation to assess 
whether the MiCAR remains ‘fit for purpose’ following its initial implementation and 
recent market developments. In September, the ECB, alongside the 27 EU national 
central banks, submitted a recommendation to drop the requirement for stablecoin 
issuers to hold deposits at commercial banks. 
Instead, the European System of Central Banks proposes implementing a minimum 
percentage of assets maturing within one to five working days to be held by the 
stablecoin issuer. This is said to lower both concentration and spillover risks. However, it 
could further accentuate the impact on banks’ funding by transforming a funding shift 
into a net deposit outflow. 
Stablecoin and, more generally, crypto asset regulations are still emerging globally. 
Other regulators have highlighted the risks that links between stablecoins and the 
banking sector could create. The most striking example comes from the UK, where the 
Bank of England announced that it aims to ban stablecoin issuers from holding reserves 
at commercial banks, specifically to mitigate spillover risks in case of a shock. As 
stablecoin usage continues to rise, we can expect further changes to the regulatory 
landscape globally. 
Competing through innovation 
Despite the lack of international harmonisation, current regulations restrict stablecoin 
issuers from directly distributing remuneration to holders. This represents a significant 
regulatory advantage for the banking sector, as it limits the use of the crypto asset to 
transactional purposes. Remuneration distribution is therefore a central discussion point 
for stablecoin issuers, as removing the ban would allow them to position their product 
not only as a means of payment but also as a store of value. 2027 will be a crucial year 
as negotiations surrounding the US Clarity Act continue, potentially allowing stablecoin 
issuers to directly distribute rewards. While still far from an agreement, such a 
derogation would be a first globally and could open the door for similar changes in other 
jurisdictions. To counter this competition, banks could increase interest rates on their 
deposit accounts; however, this would have an impact on their profitability. 
Therefore, the critical response to the rise of stablecoins lies instead in innovation. 
Indeed, crypto assets’ main advantage lies in their speed and limited costs, but banks 
are already showing they can innovate to compete. The European payment landscape 
is relatively cheap and fast despite being heavily fragmented. Some national payment 
systems exist, but a large share of transactions are processed through non-EU providers. 
4

### Page 30

Bank Sector Outlook 2027 October 2026

Stablecoins’ promise to process payments (nearly) instantly and at extremely low costs, 
even across borders, could put pressure on existing systems. However, banks and 
payment system providers are already exploring ways to compete and experimenting 
with new digital transaction methods. 
One example lies in tokenisation. Tokenised assets are digital representations of real-
world assets on a distributed ledger infrastructure. A wide range of assets can be 
tokenised, but most banks are experimenting with deposit tokenisation. These could 
modernise payment and settlement infrastructures as well as intragroup liquidity 
management. They promise faster settlement, 24/7 transactions, programmability and 
lower operational frictions. Additionally, tokenised deposits offer regulatory advantages, 
as they remain fully within the existing supervisory framework. The underlying assets 
stay on the commercial banks’ balance sheet, preserving the same oversight and 
prudential standards applied to traditional deposits. 
Banks’ competitive response could also extend to the regulatory field. The sector could 
push regulators to implement or maintain strict rules for the crypto asset sector to 
adequately reflect their risks. The US provides us with a striking example, as negotiations 
for the Clarity Act have been ongoing for months, with financial institutions pressing the 
importance of the ‘same risk, same regulation’ principle. While the final version of the US 
crypto landscape regulation is still to be agreed upon, US banks have played a central 
role in the negotiations by requesting proportionate safeguards surrounding the use of 
crypto assets. 
Banks' role in stablecoin adoption 
While we have explored in depth the impact stablecoins could have on the banking 
sector upon broad adoption, it’s important to highlight that banks also play a role in 
the crypto asset’s adoption. A central variable in determining the future adoption and 
development of stablecoins is the degree to which they are integrated into existing 
payment systems. Greater integration not only increases accessibility for a broader 
range of customers but also improves the ease of use. This is especially relevant as 
stablecoins are still mainly used in the crypto landscape and by digitally savvy users. 
Banks could therefore support the adoption of stablecoins for retail, day-to-day 
payments by integrating the asset into their digital systems or creating their own 
tokens, adding it as a new payment rail. Several examples in the EU and the US show 
the role banks can play in issuing their own stablecoins, defending their place in the 
payment landscape, even when it comes to new technologies. By issuing their own 
stablecoins, banks could also avoid losing client relationships and reduce the risk of 
becoming commodity providers for new payment issuers. Beyond this, banks are also 
exploring ways in which stablecoin integration could improve the customer experience 
while increasing fee revenues. 
Looking specifically at Europe, banks’ stablecoin issuance could also play a significant 
role in the growth of EUR-denominated stablecoins. To date, USD-pegged stablecoins 
represent a striking 99% of all stablecoins in use. The widespread use of USD stablecoins 
in the EU would have significant monetary implications on top of those discussed here 
for the banking sector. Over the past few years, the European Union has increasingly 
focused on its ‘strategic autonomy’ agenda. Part of the discussion concerns the Union’s 
significant reliance on non-EU payment services, primarily US-based ones. The 
development of EUR-denominated stablecoins by local banks could provide an answer 
to these concerns, allowing for the development of a fast, cost-efficient European 
payment system directly competing with non-EU alternatives. 
5

### Page 31

Bank Sector Outlook 2027 October 2026

Potentially significant implications, but banks are unlikely to 
surrender ground to fintech stablecoin issuers 
The broad adoption of stablecoins could have a significant impact on European banks. 
These range from a net drop in retail deposits or a shift towards wholesale funding to 
greater concentration and spillover risks, as well as an overall negative effect on credit 
provision and potentially banks’ business models. 
While it remains important to continue exploring these risks as stablecoin use grows, it’s 
also crucial to highlight that the banking sector has tools at its disposal to not only 
counter them, but also benefit from the underlying technology. Indeed, one should not 
expect the “traditional” financial sector to simply stand aside as crypto assets continue 
to gain traction. 
Despite significant growth over the past five years, stablecoins remain far from reaching 
widespread adoption. In our previous publication (read more here), we identified several 
factors that could shape their future. For banks, the key question is how to compete with 
the benefits stablecoins offer. The sector is already making substantial progress in 
instant-payment technologies and exploring digital alternatives (such as tokenisation) 
that could provide similar benefits with fewer regulatory concerns. These initiatives 
could significantly reduce stablecoins’ relative appeal. Furthermore, it’s essential to 
remember that banks still play a central role in payments and could also play a crucial 
role in the uptake of stablecoins by issuing their own tokens. This includes influencing 
which currency-pegged stablecoins gain traction and how they are integrated into the 
broader financial system. 
Next year promises to deliver another wave of stablecoin-related news. In the EU, the 
MiCAR review could translate into a change in the reserve framework, a key issue when 
assessing the potential impact of stablecoins on banks. In the UK, 2027 will also be an 
important year as it will see the enforcement of its comprehensive stablecoin regime in 
October. The US must also still finalise its CLARITY Act, potentially allowing stablecoin 
issuers to directly distribute remuneration to holders. This would be a world first and a 
major shift in the regulatory treatment of the crypto asset, opening the door to similar 
changes in other countries. 
While the upcoming regulatory changes could alter the potential impact of stablecoin 
adoption on the banking sector, we should not expect banks to surrender ground to 
fintech stablecoin issuers without a fight.

### Page 32

Bank Sector Outlook 2027 October 2026

On 30 January 2027, the amendments to the Solvency II regime will apply, making 
these revisions one of the important regulatory themes for 2027. One of the key 
objectives of the Solvency II reforms is to release capital and promote long-term 
investments by European insurance companies in support of the ambitions of Europe’s 
Savings and Investments Union (SIU). Such capital release could also increase the 
capacity of insurance companies to invest more in corporate and bank bonds. 
During the past decade, insurance companies have reduced their bond holdings in 
favour of equity. Rather than an active reallocation of assets, this partially reflects 
second-round effects related to the strong equity market growth and the rise in bond 
yields and corresponding decline in bond valuations against the backdrop of the ECB 
rate hikes in 2022 and 2023. Higher yields have also coincided in recent years with a 
stronger focus by banks on issuance in shorter maturities, rather than the longer 
maturities traditionally favoured by insurance companies. This has been particularly 
noteworthy for covered bonds. 
We don’t expect the Solvency II revisions to reverse this trend, particularly as the 
amendments promote long-term equity investments. Instead, for most bank bonds, the 
spread-risk capital treatment remains broadly unchanged. Unsecured and subordinated 
bank bonds continue to be treated as corporate bonds with capital charges determined 
by their ratings and duration, while qualifying covered bonds keep their preferential 
treatment. The impact of the Solvency II revisions therefore arises mainly indirectly 
through lower overall capital requirements, while securitisations are the only debt 
instruments benefiting directly through a more favourable capital treatment. 
Nonetheless, certain reforms are worth highlighting for their potential impact on 
insurance demand for different bank bonds. Among these, the amendments relating to

Maureen Schuller 
Head of Financials Sector Strategy 
maureen.schuller@ing.com

Solvency II reforms offer limited 
support for bank bonds, but 
securitisation gains some ground 
The Solvency II amendments are unlikely to result in a material increase in demand for 
bank bonds. Securitisations benefit most directly through lower capital charges, while 
covered bonds could benefit modestly from the new liquidity-buffer rules

### Page 33

Bank Sector Outlook 2027 October 2026

long-term equity investments, securitisations and the volatility adjustment are 
particularly noteworthy. 
Insurance companies increase equity versus debt exposures (€bn)

Source: EIOPA, ING

Long-term equity investments and non-life liquidity buffers 
Under Solvency II, insurance companies can classify part of their equity holdings as 
long-term equity investments (LTEI), benefiting from a lower 22% capital charge. To 
qualify, these investments must be held for at least five years, be listed in the EEA or 
OECD, meet diversification standards and be managed under a long-term investment 
policy. The insurer must also be able to avoid forced sales for five years, even under 
stressed conditions. 
The Solvency II amended Delegated Regulation introduces two approaches to 
demonstrate that forced equity sales can be avoided. 1) The insurer must either have 
sufficient illiquid homogeneous life insurance liabilities with a duration of more than 9.5 
years or a liquidity buffer exceeding 100% for non-life insurance obligations. 2) The 
insurer can also apply a forced-selling test to prove that it can generate cash inflows 
that are consistently higher than the cash outflows over five years, including under 
stress. 
The liquidity buffer requirements for non-life insurance activities are similar to the 
Liquidity Coverage Ratio (LCR) requirements for banks. The portfolio of liquid assets can 
comprise Level 1, Level 2A and Level 2B assets, with the total value of Level 2A and Level 
2B assets capped at 40% and the value of the Level 2B assets at 15%. 
• Qualifying Level 1 assets, subject to a 0% haircut, are cash, exposures to the ECB, 
multilateral development banks and international organisations, and domestic 
currency exposures to EEA central governments and central banks. 
• Qualifying Level 2A assets, subject to a 15% haircut, are bonds and loans rated AA- 
or better that are not issued by an insurance company or bank, and covered bonds 
rated AA- or better as referred to in Article 3(1) of the European Covered Bond 
Directive (CBD) (ie ‘European Covered Bonds’). 
• Qualifying Level 2B assets, subject to a 25% haircut, are Simple, Transparent and 
Standardised (STS) securitisations (not originated by group entities) rated AA- or 
better or that constitute a senior tranche; or subject to a 50% haircut, bonds and 
loans rated A+ to BBB- that are not issued by an insurance company or bank, and 
equity investments listed in the EEA or OECD, or traded on multilateral trading 
facilities, that are not long-term or strategic equity investments and are not 
investments in an insurance company or bank.

500
1,000
1,500

Government bonds Covered bonds Corporate bonds
Subordinated bonds Equity

### Page 34

Bank Sector Outlook 2027 October 2026

While unsecured bank bonds are excluded as eligible liquid assets, the LTEI-related 
liquidity buffer approach could generate some additional demand for covered bonds or 
STS securitisations of banks. However, we don’t expect this effect to be significant given 
the relatively small share of non-life insurance obligations and insurers’ sizeable existing 
portfolios of other liquid assets, such as government bonds. 
Insurers have limited investments in covered bonds or collateralised securities

Source: EIOPA, ING

The LTEI liquidity approach is separate from the generic liquidity and concentration risk 
management requirements under Solvency II, as well as from the obligation under the 
amended Solvency II Directive to provide for a liquidity risk management plan. 
Lower capital requirements for securitisations 
The amended Solvency II Delegated Regulation lowers the capital requirements for 
securitisation notes. This should encourage insurers to invest more in these transactions, 
helping banks to transfer risk and expand lending. 
• The risk factors (ai and bi) for the spread risk of senior tranches of STS securitisations 
will become fully aligned with the treatment of similarly rated covered bonds as 
referred to in CBD Article 3(1). 
• The risk factors for non-senior STS securitisations are reduced to a similar extent, 
with the new capital charges for AAA-rated non-senior notes at c.70% of the current 
capital charge, and for AA-rated non-senior notes at 75% of the current requirement. 
• The difference in the capital treatment between STS and non-STS securitisations is 
currently far more significant for insurers than for banks. This difference will be 
reduced substantially, while the capital charge remains capped at 100%. 
• The current Solvency II regime makes no distinction between senior and non-senior 
non-STS securitisation notes, exaggerating the spread risk of senior compared with 
non-senior tranches. Hence, lower risk factors are introduced for senior non-STS 
compared to non-senior non-STS transactions. 
As a result of these amendments, the difference in capital requirements between CBD-
compliant covered bonds and senior STS securitisations will disappear from the end of 
January, while in the highest rating buckets (AAA and AA) securitisations will be treated 
better than unsecured bonds or third-country covered bonds. Further down the rating 
scale, STS securitisations will be treated comparably to unsecured bonds from a capital 
perspective, where corporate and bank unsecured bonds now benefit from a more 
favourable capital charge compared to similarly rated senior STS securitisations.

Securitisation alternatives will become more competitive from a capital perspective

Source: European Commission, ING

European insurance companies currently hold only about €37bn in collateralised debt, 
such as asset-backed securities (ABS) and residential mortgage-backed securities 
(RMBS). This is only 12% of what they hold in covered bonds and 3% of their corporate 
debt portfolio. 
The Solvency II amendments may motivate insurance companies to diversify their debt 
portfolios a bit further away from covered bonds and other bank bond products towards 
securitisations. However, the impact is not expected to be substantial given that the 
weighted average lives of securitisation notes may not always be well suited to the 
longer-term investment needs of insurance companies. 
Volatility adjustment to align spread sensitivities more closely 
Insurance companies calculate the best estimate of their liabilities by discounting the 
expected future liability cash flows using the relevant risk-free interest rate term 
structure. To mitigate the balance sheet impact of bond spread volatility (ie, on the 
asset side of their balance sheet), insurance companies can discount their liabilities at 
the risk-free rate plus a volatility adjustment (VA).

The volatility adjustment increases the discount rate 
Spot rate, %

Source: EIOPA, ING

For each relevant currency (or national market in the case of the euro area), the 
volatility adjustment represents 65% of the spread (S) between the interest rate on the 
reference portfolio and the relevant risk-free interest rate, adjusted for a risk correction 
(RC). The latter correction factor removes the part of the spread that is attributable to 
expected losses or unexpected credit or other risks to the assets. The volatility 
3

### Page 36

Bank Sector Outlook 2027 October 2026

adjustment is only added to the applicable risk-free interest rates that are not derived 
from extrapolation. 
For each relevant country, a country-specific adjustment may also be added to the 
volatility adjustment before the application of the 65% factor if a) the risk-corrected 
country spread is more than twice the risk-corrected currency spread, and b) the risk-
corrected country spread is more than 85bp. The country component in the volatility 
adjustment helps mitigate excessive widening in specific countries. 
Under the current regime, the representative currency and country portfolios are 
constructed as uniform portfolios, even though insurers’ actual fixed-income portfolios 
may differ substantially. Some insurers can therefore benefit excessively from the 
volatility adjustment if the resulting reduction in the best estimate of their liabilities 
exceeds the decline in the value of the relevant assets when credit spreads widen. 
For this reason, the Solvency II revisions change the calculation of the volatility 
adjustment by a) making the applicable risk corrections (RC) more spread-sensitive, and 
b) introducing a credit spread sensitivity ratio (CSSR) to better align the volatility 
adjustment with the insurer’s asset-liability profile. The latter ratio reflects the price 
value of a basis point of the insurer’s bond, securitisation and loan investments versus 
the price value of a basis point of the best estimate of liabilities, with a minimum of 0 
and a maximum of 1. 
Moreover, subject to approval by the supervisory authority, an undertaking-specific 
adjustment (USA) of up to 105% may be applied to align the risk-corrected spread on 
the reference portfolio more closely with that of the insurer’s own portfolio. This must 
not lead to an increase in the applied risk-adjusted spread for more than two 
consecutive quarters. Moreover, when the USA is used, the insurer cannot also increase 
the volatility adjustment through a macro volatility adjustment. 
The macro volatility adjustment replaces the existing country-specific adjustment and 
can be added to the euro volatility adjustment when the relevant conditions are met. It 
aims to mitigate the impact of excessive spread widening in individual euro area 
countries and is based on the difference between the risk-corrected euro spread and the 
risk-corrected spread for the relevant country. 
Volatility adjustment calculations

Subscripts cu = currency, co = country, ma = macro; VA = volatility adjustment, S = spread of the reference 
portfolio, RC = risk correction, RCS = risk-corrected spread of the reference portfolio, CSSR = credit spread 
sensitivity ratio, USA = undertaking-specific adjustment. 
Source: European Commission, ING

The introduction of additional minimum conditions, such as supervisory approval and 
the availability of adequate processes to calculate the volatility adjustment, has also 
facilitated the upward revision of the general application ratio (GAR) from 65% to 85%.

### Page 37

Bank Sector Outlook 2027 October 2026

Primarily through the introduction of the credit spread sensitivity ratio (CSSR), the 
volatility adjustment will better reflect the relationship between each insurer’s asset and 
liability sensitivity to changes in the volatility adjustment. The revision may provide 
modest support for bonds that increase the asset portfolio’s spread sensitivity, as a 
higher CSSR results in a larger volatility adjustment until the ratio reaches its cap of one. 
The revised calculations should also reduce the possibility for some insurers to benefit 
disproportionately from the volatility adjustment when their assets are less spread-
sensitive than the reference portfolio. 
Conclusion 
The Solvency II amendments are unlikely to trigger a material reallocation in favour of 
bank bonds. Securitisations are the most obvious beneficiaries of a more favourable 
capital treatment, while covered bonds and unsecured bank bonds should mainly 
benefit indirectly from lower overall capital requirements for insurers. We therefore 
expect the revisions to only support insurer demand for bank bonds at the margin.

Bank Sector Outlook 2027 October 2026

Style Exposure
We are incrementally positive on Jentech with multiple catalysts ahead. Near-term,

we believe the company’s 3Q26 earnings could be the first driver with a potential
GM beat, which should be reported in the second week of Nov. Longer-term, we
believe the drivers are from the incremental adoption of the removable lid in some
Nvidia Rubin GPUs in 2027 (in addition to Rubin Ultra), followed by the
meaningful Google TPU v9 spec upgrade potential in 2028 as another multi-fold
content increase story (based on JPMe), along with a structural margin expansion

trend given continued GPU and ASIC upgrades. Thus, we revise up our 2027E and
2028E EPS materially and are now 28% and 18% above the current BBGe
consensus.
• 3Q26 preview: expecting a stronger GM growth; 4Q26 outlook: slightly
better rev growth. We revise up our 3Q26 GM to 50.1% (vs current BBGe
consensus at 48.7%), which implies another quarter of strong GM expansion
(vs 2Q/1Q26 GM at 46.8%/41.8%), driven by better product mix to more AI
server business. Reported 3Q26 revenue of NT$9.5bn was largely in line with

the company’s guidance, and the 30% QoQ growth was mainly driven by the
decent ramp of Rubin GPU. As the Rubin 1-piece lid shipment should remain
at high volume into 4Q26, we believe the company’s revenue momentum will
also continue, with incremental growth from small volume of removable lid
shipment. Hence, we are now modeling Jentech’s 4Q26 revenue to grow 3%
QoQ, with GM further expanding thanks to ongoing mix enhancement.
• Further 2027 upside from incremental Rubin removable lid adoption;

2028 another earnings level-up year driven by meaningful upgrade in
TPU v9.We revise up our 2027E and 2028E revenue estimates materially by
22% and 14%, which are now 24% and 16% above consensus, respectively. For
the 2027 upward revision, it mainly reflects the incremental adoption of
removable lid (3-piece lid) in partial Rubin GPU from the next 1-2 quarter, in
addition to Rubin Ultra, as long as the company can ramp up its yield and
production of removable lid in the next few months with on-schedule capacity

expansion (new Taoyuan Aerotropolis plant) and equipment move-in (e.g. the
in-house screw locking equipment). More encouragingly, we believe 2028 will
see even greater earnings upside with Google TPU v9 likely upgrading its heat
spreader solution significantly to 2-piece or 3-piece lid design, in our view, as
the potential chip size (4-die) and TDP (potentially 2k+W or even higher)
should be a big level-up compared to v8, which makes the current stiffener-

only solution insufficient. As the content of 2-piece or 3-piece lid solution
could be 5-10x higher than the v8 stiffener, Google TPU v9 (with meaningful
volume) could drive the next wave of the earnings upgrade cycle for Jentech
from 2028 (following Nvidia Rubin/Rubin Ultra as the major driver in 2027).
• Implications. We lift our 2027E and 2028E EPS by 28% and 17% to NT$193
and NT$295, respectively, reflecting the incremental removable lid adoption

Sources for: Style Exposure – J.P. Morgan Global Markets Strategy; all other tables are company data and J.P. Morgan estimates.

Summary Investment Thesis and Valuation
Investment Thesis
Jentech (OW) is the largest packaging-level cooling component
maker worldwide, with leading production capabilities across
heat spreader products (including stiffener and all kinds of lids).
We expect the company to show a ~90% EPS CAGR over 2024-
28, driven by solid heat spreader content growth in future-gen AI

chips and increasing TAM into new solutions (e.g., CPO switch
ASIC). In addition, the company is reinforcing its product
portfolio with new offerings in cold plate and CPU server
sockets for chip customers. We believe Jentech’s technology
leadership in the structural cooling upgrade trend and its new
TAM expansion will continue to support its superior earnings
power in the long term.
Valuation
Our Dec-27 PT of NT$8,800 is based on 30x 2028E P/E, which

is largely in line with Jentech’s three-year average P/E when the
AI server boom started.

Investment Thesis, Valuation and Risks
Jentech Precision Industrial Co. (Overweight; Price Target: NT
$8,800.00)
Investment Thesis
Jentech (OW) is the largest packaging-level cooling component maker worldwide, with
leading production capabilities across heat spreader products (including stiffener and all
kinds of lids). We expect the company to show a ~90% EPS CAGR over 2024-28, driven

by solid heat spreader content growth in future-gen AI chips and increasing TAM into new
solutions (e.g. CPO switch ASIC). In addition, the company is reinforcing its product
portfolio with new offerings in cold plate and CPU server sockets for chip customers. We
believe Jentech’s technology leadership in the structural cooling upgrade trend and its new
TAM expansion will continue to support its superior earnings power in the long term.
Valuation

Our Dec-27 PT of NT$8,800 is based on 30x 2028E P/E, which is largely in line with
Jentech’s three-year average P/E when the AI server boom started.
Risks to Rating and Price Target
Downside risks to our rating and price target include: 1) Potential delay in mainstream 3-
piece/removable lid design adoption, and 2) market share loss from competition.

Standard Chartered Bank

Charts of the week: Diverging volatility

Strong earnings should resolve the bond and equity market divergence; French political risks remain largely contained  
S&P500 volatility index (VIX); US bond volatility index (MOVE)*   10-year government bond yield spreads over German bonds

Source: Bloomberg, Standard Chartered; *measures US government bond volatility

Editorial 
Will earnings resolve the bond-equity split?  
Strategy summary:  Bond and equity market volatility has 
sharply diverged. Elevated bond volatility and political risks in 
the US and Europe warrant near-term caution, prompting us to 
lock in tactical gains in semiconductors and Taiwan equities. 
Our quantitative stock-bond model turned negative for the first 
time since March 2025, signalling near-term equity volatility . 
We stay invested in a diversified allocation  slightly overweight 
equities and prefer to navigate the upcoming results and macro 
events before adding high-beta exposure within equities. 
Consensus estimates point to a c. 31% y/y rise in S&P500 Q3 
earnings, driven by technology -sector earnings growth of c. 
67%. Earnings have beaten lofty estimates in H1 as the AI 
build-out exceeds expectations. This favours tech -led equities 
in the medium term. In Europe, French political and fiscal risks 
cloud the EUR outlook, but regional bank equities remain 
attractive on robust profitability, capital strength and valuations. 
Divergence between equities and bonds.  Equity markets 
signal calm, with the S&P 500 index near record highs and its 
volatility index (VIX) low at 15, while the US government bond 
volatility index (MOVE) is close to its highest since March. Bond 
stress reflects two-decade-high long yields amid strong growth, 
oil close to USD 100/bbl fuelling near -term inflation, fiscal 
uncertainty and crowded investor short positioning vulnerable 
to abrupt unwinds. High bond volatility could spill into equities. 
Our quantitative stock-bond model turns negative. For the 
first time since March 2025, the model score has turned 
negative, driven by deteriorating equity market breadth and 
above-average risk aversion. History shows global equity 
volatility rises most in the month after the score dips to -1. 
However, equities still outperformed bonds over the following 
1-12 months, on average, despite higher market risk. 
Taking some chips off the table; locking in gains:  Rising 
bond volatility, the negative stock -bond model signal, weak 
equity breadth and approaching US mid-term elections warrant 
near-term caution. US small -caps remain about 9% below 
August’s all-time highs even as large-caps reach new records. 
The macro uncertainty prompted us to lock in gains in 
semiconductor and Taiwan equity opportunistic ideas. 
Strong Q3 earnings season likely to overcome near -term 
challenges. Strong US and Asian earnings should ultimately 
resolve the equity -bond divergence in favour of stocks. 
Consensus S&P 500 Q3 earnings growth has risen to 30 .6% 
y/y, while technology is tracking 66.5%, driven by the AI 
infrastructure buildout. AI investment plans, monetisation and 
funding needs are key metrics to watch. Record memory -chip 
profits and higher long -term targets from AI leaders support 
staying long technology-led risk assets and using near -term 
volatility to scale into preferred sectors. Also, US equities have 
historically performed strongly in the 12 months after mid -term 
elections, particularly when the opposition regains control of the 
House and the Senate – as seems increasingly likely. 
Medium-term constructive on equities. Beyond Q3 earnings, 
we expect US earnings to rise 15-20% next year. Almost half of 
the estimated USD 420 per share of S&P500 earnings in 2027 
should come from tech-related sectors: information technology, 
communication services and consumer discretionary. We also 
favour US, Europe and Japan financial sectors as solid growth 
and higher rates lift lending income, while the AI buildout boosts 
advisory fees from IPOs and M&A s. However, a nother 30-
50bps rise in long-term bond yields , without earnings growth 
upgrades, could be a  headwind for equities.  Next week’s US 
inflation data for September is the next catalyst for bond yields. 
French uncertainty challenges near -term EUR view; 
continue to see opportunities in Euro area bank equities.  
Rising French political and fiscal risks have largely remained 
confined to France, with its bond yield spread versus German 
peers surging to the widest since the 2011-12 Euro area crisis. 
Nevertheless, EUR/USD has fallen around 4% since mid -
August, and our three-month forecast of 1.15 is under review. 
Tighter financial conditions and political uncertainty in France 
and Spain raises the risk of a n ECB rate hold. Euro area bank 
equities remain attractive on profitability, capital strength and 
valuations, subject to sovereign-spread and funding-cost risks.  
—  Rajat Bhattacharya and Sundeep Gantori

Standard Chartered Bank 
WS Global CIO Office | 9 October 2026

Top client questions 
  What are the market implications of France's political and 
fiscal uncertainty?   
Our view: France’s political and fiscal uncertainty remain near-
term headwinds for European assets. We are placing our three-
month EUR/USD forecast (1.15) under review for a potential 
downward adjustment, while retaining our 12-month forecast at 
1.18 as broader Euro  area spillovers remain limited. Our base 
case remains one more ECB hike in December followed by a 
prolonged pause, although tighter financial conditions increase 
the risk of a delay. For equities, we retain Euro area banks as 
an Opportunistic idea, supported by strong profitability, capital 
buffers and reasonable valuations. For bank bonds, we prefer a 
subordinated capital structure to senior debt. 
Rationale: France’s 10-year government bond yield has risen amid 
renewed political and fiscal concerns. This has tightened financial 
conditions and added to the near-term EUR risk premium. Together 
with elevated US yields, this raises downside risks to our three-
month EUR/USD view. However, the stress has not yet developed 
into broad Euro area contagion, and recent expectations of progress 
on France’s 2027 budget have provided some relief. Broader Euro  
area growth and labour  market fundamentals also remain relatively 
resilient, supporting our unchanged 12-month EUR/USD view. 
For the ECB, higher sovereign yields are already delivering some 
monetary tightening. Markets have consequently pared expectations 
of additional ECB hikes. We continue to expect one more hike in 
December, given elevated near -term inflation, but persistent fiscal 
or political stress in France and Spain could delay further tightening. 
For Euro area banks, wider sovereign spreads  are likely to  raise 
funding costs and pressure bond portfolios, while fewer ECB hikes 
would moderate further gains in net interest income. Nevertheless, 
profitability remains strong, capital buffers are healthy and price-
to-book valuations remain reasonable, with returns on equity also 
improving. We, therefore, maintain Euro area banks as an 
Opportunistic idea , while monitoring sovereign spreads, funding 
costs and any deterioration in loan-loss provisions.  
On the bond side, European bank junior subordinated bond prices 
fell by an average of 3-5 cash points before rebounding, with French 
bank subordinated bonds underperforming those of other European 
peers. As fundamentals remain strong, we view pullbacks as 
opportunities to add and maintain our preference for a subordinated 
capital structure over senior debt for European bank bonds. 
—  Vincent Tan, Senior Investment Strategist 
— Fook Hien Yap, Senior Investment Strategist  
 Ray Heung, Senior Investment Strategist

### Page 5

Standard Chartered Bank 
WS Global CIO Office | 9 October 2026

Top client questions (cont’d) 
  What is the likelihood of elevated long -end government 
bond yields evolving into a systemic risk for the financial sector 
and public finances? 
Our view: We believe s ystemic risk remains a tail risk rather 
than a base case in the near term. The probability would rise 
materially if rollover stress, foreign demand withdrawal and 
private credit deterioration occur in combination. 
Rationale: Higher bond yields worsen fiscal deficits and drive higher 
issuance requirements, exerting further upward pressure on yields. 
The resulting tighter financial conditions weigh on economic growth. 
This feedback loop is most acute in Developed Markets (DMs) where 
debt burdens are already elevated. For instance, projected 2026 
debt-to-GDP ratios stand at 204% for Japan, 138% for Italy, 126% 
for the US, 118% for France and 104% for the UK. Countries with 
shorter average debt maturities face the sharpest near-term 
repricing risk  as maturing obligations are refinanced at current 
rates. Meanwhile, evidence suggests that  a positive equity-bond 
correlation increases the sensitivity of yields to bond supply.  
Taking the US as an example, there has been no buyers’ strike on 
US government bonds yet , though foreign demand has softened, 
narrowing the margin of safety. The US government has shifted 
towards shorter-dated issuance to manage funding costs (reducing 
near-term expenses, but concentrating rollover risk). Economic 
resilience remains evident, underpinned by corporate confidence 
in AI -driven investment . The key question is whether this 
expansion can persist long enough to stabilise the fiscal trajectory. 
Within the financial sector, corporate balance sheets  remain 
strong, while High-Yield (HY) bond and leveraged loan default rates 
remain contained, though this reflects the lag between rate rises and 
credit deterioration, not immunity. Following the failure of the Silicon 
Valley Bank, liquidity discipline among banks has improved, but 
duration mismatches persist.  
In private credit, default rates are trending upward while the asset 
class has come under continued scrutiny , with negative headlines 
and a rise in redemptions trigger ing fund gating  at some  private 
credit managers since Q 1 2026. Hence, continued monitoring of 
default rates, redemptions and potential stress events in private 
credit remains essential . That said, higher rates could buoy 
medium-term returns and distribution yields in the asset class , 
as the majority of direct lending loans have a floating-rate structure. 
—  Ray Heung, Senior Investment Strategist

ratios exceeding 100%

Standard Chartered Bank

Top client questions (cont’d) 
  What is your outlook for the US Q3 2026 earnings season, 
especially for the banking sector? 
Our view: We expect another quarter of robust US earnings 
growth to support our Overweight stance on US equities. We 
see m ajor US banks, which begin reporting next week, also 
delivering healthy results, reinforcing our Overweight view on  
the US financial sector. 
Rationale: Consensus forecasts S&P500 Q3 earnings growth  of 
30.6% y/y, up from 27.6% at the start of July. Energy is expected to 
post the strongest growth at 114.7%, supported by elevated oil 
prices amid ongoing geopolitical tensions. Information technology 
(66.5%) and communication services (49.0%) remain key 
earnings drivers, benefiting from sustained AI -related demand and 
investment. The broader outlook remains supportive, with full-year 
earnings growth projected at 35.8% in 2026 and 15.6% in 2027. 
For banks, the recent underperformance amid concerns over higher 
long-term bond yields has, in our view, improved the forward-looking 
risk-reward profile.  Over the medium term, strengthening loan 
growth and resilient credit quality should continue to support solid 
fundamentals. Capital markets activity also remains constructive, 
with industry leaders well -positioned to benefit from their strong 
investment banking and trading franchises. 
—  Jason Wong, Senior Equity Analyst 
  How could upcoming semiconductor manufacturing and 
equipment earnings reshape the AI capex outlook? 
Our view: We maintain our AI capex outlook as robust demand 
and ongoing supply constraints support a multi-year expansion 
cycle. In the near term, however, we take a more prudent stance 
on semiconductors amid an expected rise in equity volatility. 
Rationale: Recent memory industry guidance points to sustained 
investment in advanced chip manufacturing , with customer 
commitments extending through 2031. Upcoming earnings should 
provide visibility into capacity expansion plans. This supports our AI 
capex outlook, which we will monitor through the earnings season.  
That said, we expect equity volatility to rise in the near term amid 
elevated long-term yields and macroeconomic uncertainty. Recent 
media reports on AI lab revenues  have also led to doubts over 
monetisation and the ability to sustain elevated capex spending. We 
thus took profit on our Global Semiconductors (8.1% return from 9-
Sep-26 to 2-Oct-26) and MSCI Taiwan (10.0% return from 9-Jul-26 to 
2-Oct-26) Opportunistic ideas following strong performance. This does 
not reflect a deterioration in fundamentals, but rather a need to be 
prudent on high-beta exposure. We remain constructive on the AI 
theme over the long term. 
—  Ryan Goh, Investment Strategist

### Page 7

Standard Chartered Bank 
WS Global CIO Office | 9 October 2026

Top client questions (cont’d) 
  USD/CHF has rebounded sharply over the past two 
months. What are the key factors behind the move, and do you 
see the currency pair continuing to move higher? 
Our view:  USD/CHF tested 0.84 amid strong momentum from 
the USD, but we see the pair remain ing supported near -term 
before gradually moving lower towards 0.82 in three months. 
Rationale: The Fed’s relatively high policy rate continues to support 
USD carry demand , while the Swiss National Bank maintains its 
policy rate at 0%. In the near term, however, the Swiss franc (CHF) 
could benefit from increased safe-haven demand amid mounting 
fiscal concerns in France  and political uncertainty in Spain , 
particularly if European risk premia continue to rise. Beyond these 
cyclical factors, Switzerland’s substantial net foreign asset position, 
persistent current account surpluses and low public debt burden 
continue to underpin the CHF’s structural strength . Over the 
medium term, we expect the USD’s rate advantage to gradually 
diminish as the Fed approaches the end of its tightening cycle, while 
the CHF’s defensive characteristics should remain supportive.  
—  Vincent Tan, Senior Investment Strategist 
—  Iris Yuen, Investment Strategist 
  Do you see further upside for USD/JPY after recent dovish 
comments from BoJ officials and latest bond auctions? 
Our view: We see some near-term upside risk to USD/JPY from 
elevated US  yields, but retain our medium -term constructive 
JPY view. USD/JPY remains below 159, suggesting markets are 
mindful of intervention risk as the pair approaches 160. We 
continue to expect further BoJ normalisation. 
Rationale: Recent BoJ communication appears less dovish than the 
market narrative had suggested. BoJ Governor Ueda continues to 
emphasise the need to anchor underlying inflation around 2%  
and gradually reduce monetary accommodation, supporting our view 
that the tightening cycle is not yet over. An October pause would 
reflect a more measured pace of policy normalisation  rather than a 
change in the BoJ’s policy direction. 
Recent Japanese government bond (JGB) auctions have shown 
resilient demand despite higher yields.  At the latest 10 -year JGB 
auction, the bid -to-cover ratio improved  to 3.76x from 3.29x 
previously, while the auction tail (spread between highest 
accepted yield and issued yield) narrowed to 0.2bps from 1.6bps. 
We see this as indicative of an orderly, functioning market rather than 
a dovish BoJ signal . Intervention concerns around  the 160 level 
should help limit near-term USD/JPY upside.  
—  Vincent Tan, Senior Investment Strategist 
—  Iris Yuen, Investment Strategist

USD/CHF gains driven by higher US bond yields 
are likely to be offset by CHF safe-haven demand

長榮航太 2645 TT  ( 航運業 ) 
長空展翼，航向新高 
投資與評價 
1. 長榮航太 3Q26 受惠維修需求穩健，營收維持季增，惟製造待料、原物料
成本偏高及管理費用增加壓抑獲利，預估 EPS 1.90 元；4Q26 維修需求與
製造出貨動能延續，惟年終獎金及相關人事費用增加，EPS 預估 1.77 元。 
2. 26 年受惠維修工時費率調升、既有機隊延長使用，獲利預估創歷史新高；
27 年維修業務透過人力補充與周轉率改善 ， 製造業務則受惠發動機零組件
需求及空巴 A321 新案投產 ， 帶動營運續創新高 ， 預估26 / 27 年 EPS 8.30 
/ 9.40 元。 
3. 目前長榮航太股價 170.5 元，約為 18.1 倍 FY27(F) PER，位於近三年 15-
35 倍本益比區間中間偏下位置。考量維修業務需求穩定，製造產能維持滿
載。製造業務受惠波音 737 MAX 10 重回民航市場，惟無人機業務仍需等
待，維持買進投資評等，因調整 26、27 年獲利預估，目標價調高至 210
元(22x 27(F)PER)。 
重點更新 
長榮航太 3Q26 維修需求支撐，成本增加但營收維持成長 
3Q26 維修業務受惠棚廠持續接近滿載 ， 新機交付未能完全滿足航空公司需求 ，
帶動既有機隊延長使用年限，支撐定期檢修及結構修復業務。製造業務則受惠
發動機零組件需求增加，但原料到貨時間不穩定，影響生產排程與出貨效率，
營收增加尚未充分轉化為獲利改善。 3Q26 營收 52.75 億元(+1.0% QoQ 、
+17.5% YoY)，優於先前預估 3.9%。惟製造業務原物料成本仍高，下調毛利率
預估至 21.4%(+0.1ppts QoQ) ；因上調管理費用預估，營業費用上調至 2.45
億元(+10.1% QoQ)，營業利益 8.82 億元(-0.9% QoQ)。預估稅後淨利 7.11 億
元(-5.6% QoQ)，EPS 1.90 元，營收較去年同期維持成長。 
長榮航太 4Q26 營運受人事成本壓抑，本業營運創歷史同期新高 
4Q26 維修業務受惠既有機隊檢修需求及工時費率調整，棚廠維持滿載。製造
業務隨客戶生產需求增加，維持出貨動能另外，第四季通常認列年終獎金及相
關人事成本，毛利率與營業費用率相對 3Q26 承壓。考量維修人事相關成本提
升，下調4Q26 營收預估 3.4%至 52.26 億元(-0.9% QoQ)，同步下調毛利率至
20.4%(-1.0ppts QoQ)；因預期員工福利費用與研發費用增加，上調營業費用
預估至 2.62 億元(+6.7% QoQ)，但營業利益仍年增 67.2%至 8.05 億元(-8.8% 
QoQ)。預估稅後淨利 6.64 億元(-6.7% QoQ、+39.2% YoY)，EPS 1.77 元，
本業營運創歷史同期新高。

長榮航太 3Q26 維修需求支撐，成本增加但營收維持成長 
長榮航太 3Q26 維修業務受惠棚廠持續接近滿載，新機交付未能完全滿足航空公司需求，帶動既
有機隊延長使用年限，支撐定期檢修及結構修復業務。製造業務則受惠發動機零組件需求增加，
但原料到貨時間不穩定，影響生產排程與出貨效率，營收增加尚未充分轉化為獲利改善。 
3Q26 營收 52.75 億元(+1.0% QoQ、+17.5% YoY)，優於先前預估 3.9%。惟製造業務原物料成
本仍高， 下調毛利率預估至21.4%(+0.1ppts QoQ)；因上調管理費用預估 ，營業費用上調至 2.45
億元(+10.1% QoQ)，營業利益 8.82 億元(-0.9% QoQ)。預估稅後淨利 7.11 億元(-5.6% QoQ)，
EPS 1.90 元，營收較去年同期維持成長。

長榮航太 4Q26 營運受人事成本壓抑，本業營運創歷史同期新高 
長榮航太 4Q26 維修業務受惠既有機隊檢修需求及工時費率調整，棚廠維持滿載。製造業務則
隨客戶新機與發動機生產需求增加，維持出貨動能，惟原料到貨時間不穩定，仍影響生產效率與
營收。另外，第四季通常認列年終獎金及相關人事成本，毛利率與營業費用率相對 3Q26 承壓。 
考量維修人事相關成本提升，下調 4Q26 營收預估 3.4%至 52.26 億元(-0.9% QoQ)，同步下調
毛利率至 20.4%(-1.0ppts QoQ)；因預期員工福利費用與研發費用增加，上調營業費用預估至
2.62 億元(+6.7% QoQ)，但營業利益仍年增 67.2%至 8.05 億元(-8.8% QoQ)。預估稅後淨利
6.64 億元(-6.7% QoQ、+39.2% YoY)，EPS 1.77 元，本業營運創歷史同期新高。

維修調價與業外挹注，長榮航太 26 年營運將創高 
展望 26 年，長榮航太製造業務雖隨客戶新機與發動機交付需求增加而成長，但所用原物料受烏俄
戰爭影響，產線效率及改善速度仍在緩步改善。維修業務則受惠先前逐客戶協商調整工時費率，
價格效益持續反映，航空公司延長舊機使用亦支撐維修工作量。現有棚廠接近滿載， 公司將持續
投入技術人員培訓，以利未來增加承接量推升營運。 
長榮航太 26 年營收年增 12.6%至 204.61 億元，受人事與製造成本增加，略下調毛利率預估至
22.5%；營業費用 9.06 億元(+30.3% YoY)，營業利益為 37.06 億元(+23.5% YoY)。稅後淨利預估
3.2%至 31.07 億元(+52.0% YoY)，EPS 8.30 元，營運表現創下歷史新高。

737 MAX 過認證、空巴新案帶動，長榮航太 27 年營運續寫歷史新高 
波音 737 MAX 10 近期已取得 FAA 認證，解除該機型交付的重要限制。該機型訂單逾1,550 架，
約占波音 737 系列待交訂單 31.0%，有助提升後續生產與交付能見度。長榮航太承作其中 LEAP
發動機燃燒段相關產品，應用涵蓋 737 MAX 及空巴 A320neo 系列，研究部認為，後續原廠若順
利提高產量，將有助增加零組件拉貨並改善製造產能利用率。維修業務方面，27 年營運重點轉向
人力補充、第二班安排及維修周轉率改善。現有四座棚廠接近滿載，第五座棚廠須等待桃園機場
土地分配，因此27 年營運仍以既有棚廠效率提升為主。製造業務則除既有發動機零組件外，觀音
二廠預計 2Q27 完工、3Q27 開始投單生產，承接空巴A321 機身件。長異發動機則已恢復現場作
業，將緩步重回過往水準。 
故預估 27 年營收 220.01 億元(+7.5% YoY)。 受惠維修需求穩健 、 製造出貨增加及營運效率改善 ，
預估毛利率 23.4%； 營業費用8.06 億元(-11.0% YoY)， 預估營業利益為43.48 億元(+17.3% YoY)。
稅後淨利 35.20 億元(+13.7% YoY)，EPS 9.40 元，營運續寫歷史新高。

2026/10/22

波音 737 製造回溫、無人機業務添翼，維持長榮航太買進評等 
目前長榮航太股價 170.5 元，約為18.1 倍 FY27(F) PER，位於近三年 15-35 倍本益比區間中間偏下
位置。考量波音 737 MAX 10 近期取得 FAA 認證，該機型佔波音 737 系列待交訂單約 31%，逾
1550 架，認證後也降低後續交付不確定性，長榮航太 LEAP 發動機燃燒段相關產品出貨有望增加，
加上空巴新廠亦提供長榮航太中期成長機會。考量 26 年維修業務穩健成長，加上費率調整與業外
改善支撐獲利，預估 EPS 8.30 元；27 年則由既有維修需求、製造出貨增加及費用回落帶動成長，
預估 EPS 9.40 元，營運有望持續走高。 
無人機業務方面，先前 9 月行政院通過《強化國防自主暨無人載具產業發展條例》 ，連續六年編列 
2,400 億元，每年以 400 億元為原則。政策範圍涵蓋可信任供應鏈、研發等。此外，行政院也提出
的 1,457 億元國防追加預算，其中「濱海監偵型」 、 「濱海攻擊型無人機」及「小型自殺無人艇」三
項合計 559 億元 。 長榮航太製造量能充足 ， 加上具備既有監偵型無人載具交付經驗 ， 後續將持續爭
取新案。目前國防部已完成招商說明，希望在預算通過後加快採購，於 2027 年形成初始戰力，目
前仍待國防部公布相關規格。 
考量長榮航太維修業務需求穩定，以及既有製造產能維持滿載。27 年製造業務受惠波音 737 MAX 
10 重回民航市場，無人機業務提供潛在成長機會，維持買進投資評等。惟當前國防部仍未公布無人
機採購規格，仍要等待更多採購細節釋出，本益比由接近平均值之 24 倍下調至中間偏下之 22 倍，
並以 27 年獲利預估，目標價由 195 元(24x 26(F)PER)調高至 210 元(22x 27(F)PER)。

公司簡介 
長榮航太，(Evergreen Aviation Technologies Corporation，簡稱 EGAT）於 1997 年成立，總部
位於桃園市大園區，為長榮集團旗下、長榮航空持股55.05%的航空維修與航太製造公司。主要業務
分為航空器維修及航空零組件製造兩大領域，服務涵蓋機體定期維修、飛機改裝、零組件修護，以
及機體與發動機零組件製造、加工及組裝。2Q26 營收為 52.22 億元，其中維修業務 40.90 億元
(78%)；製造業務 11.32 億元(22%)。 
維修業務方面 ， 公司取得美國聯邦航空總署 （FAA） 、 歐盟航空安全署（EASA） 及日本民航局 （JCAB）
等主管機關的維修認證，除支援長榮航空與立榮航空機隊外，亦承接國際航空公司維修需求。既有
維修機型涵蓋波音 747、767、777 及 787 等，並持續拓展新世代機型服務。依 2026 年 5 月法說
資料，公司已於 1Q26 取得 EASA 的 A350 維修認證，為拓展外部客戶及支援長榮航空預計 2027
年導入 A350 機隊預作準備。 
製造業務方面，公司既有客戶包括波音與GE Aerospace，並於 2025 年與空中巴士簽署合作合約，
成為其一階機體組件供應商，擴大國際民航製造供應鏈布局。此外，公司亦拓展電子產業設備製造
及無人機業務，增加航空維修以外的成長來源。後續營運重點包括新機型維修客戶開拓、空巴新案
導入，以及新廠產能建置與量產進度。 
轉投資方面，長榮航太持有長異發動機維修 49%、長銳航材 49%及長鑽科技 9.3%股權，均於財報
列為採權益法認列的關聯企業。長異為與 GE 合作的發動機及零件維修事業；長銳主要從事航空器
零組件維修；長鑽則提供金屬製品表面化學處理服務，補強公司在發動機、零組件修護及製造製程
的布局。

長榮航太 ESG 政策 
完善永續治理架構，強化組織韌性。長榮航太於 2023 年 11 月設立「永續發展委員會」 ，由董事長
擔任召集人、總經理擔任副召集人，並由三位獨立董事擔任委員，將永續發展與風險管理納入公司
治理架構。委員會下設執行小組，由總經理擔任執行長，帶領各部門最高主管，透過 「環境保護組、
社會共融組、公司治理組、產品服務組」四大議題組落實永續策略。2025 年委員會召開 4 次會議、
平均出席率 100%，並向董事會提報 4 次，涵蓋永續工作進度、利害關係人溝通及風險管理執行情
形 。 公司自2024 年將永續報告書編製及驗證程序納入內部控制制度 ， 並於2025 年報告書參考 IFRS 
S2 架構，深化氣候相關風險與機會的財務影響揭露。 
積極減碳與能源轉型，實踐綠色行動。長榮航太依循 TCFD 架構進行氣候風險評估與情境分析，並
於 2025 年重新辨識氣候相關風險與機會，強化財務影響量化評估。公司同年改採GHG Protocol 進
行組織型溫室氣體盤查，涵蓋範疇三並取得第三方確信。節能措施包括更換高效冰水主機、增設空
壓機廢熱回收系統及電梯電能回收，全年節能量達 3,779.34 GJ；包含太陽能建置在內的各項措施，
合計減排 1,030.78 公噸二氧化碳當量。2025 年再生能源消耗量達 5,893.53 GJ，再生能源自發自用
比例達 53.34%，並回收約 84.87 噸廢金屬再利用，持續推動能源轉型與資源循環。 
落實職安與健康管理，打造安全職場。長榮航太以「安全第一，健康優先」為原則，持續運作 ISO 
45001 職業安全衛生管理系統，涵蓋桃園、觀音及松山維修部等所有工作場所，納入 3,257 名員工
及 269 名其他工作者。2025 年共舉辦 113 場職安相關教育訓練，計 2,284 人次參與，並透過危害
辨識、風險評估、事故調查及設備改善，降低現場作業風險。健康管理方面，公司提供優於法規的健
康檢查及全職醫師駐診服務，2025 年共 998 人參與健康追蹤檢查，補助金額約 200 萬元；另推動
特殊作業體檢、異常工作負荷評估、母性健康保護、疫苗接種及肌肉骨骼健康活動，強化員工身心健
康與工作適性管理。 
提升產品品質與供應鏈永續性。長榮航太秉持「安全第一、品質至上」的政策，透過ISO 9001、AS 
9100 及 AS 9110 品質管理系統，確保維修服務與零組件製造符合航空產業要求。2025 年新增 119
家供應商，全數簽署《供應商行為準則》 ，一階供應商簽署回收率達95.37%。公司將 ESG 納入新供
應商遴選評分，權重為 15%，並完成 52 家一階供應商永續性風險問卷調查；另辦理溫室氣體盤查
教育訓練，協助供應鏈建立減碳能力。2025 年客戶滿意度平均為 8.88 分，較前一年下降，公司已
啟動滿意度管理機制，透過原因調查、權責單位改善及後續追蹤，持續精進服務品質。 
培育人才與促進社會共融 。 長榮航太持續投入專業人才培育 ，2025 年員工訓練總時數達 239,565.96
小時，平均每人約 73.55 小時，訓練參與率達 99.46%，並透過專業技術、管理及語言等課程支持員
工職涯發展。同年首次執行員工滿意度、投入度與敬業度調查，其中滿意度及敬業度分別為3.57 分
與 3.86 分，滿分均為 5 分，並依調查結果推動改善措施。產學合作方面，公司與成功大學、逢甲大
學、文化大學、朝陽科技大學及淡江大學合作，舉辦 5 場企業參訪，共 193 人參與。此外，亦透過
庇護工場商品採購、公益贊助、捐血活動、社區互動及藝文支持，擴大企業對人才培育與社會共融的
投入。

## Document 480: インドネシア中銀、市場動揺も、政策運営は政府と市場の「板挟み」が続く ～一部の利上げ観測に「ゼロ回答」、様々な政策目標実_a6041851

Source PDF: all report pdf/インドネシア中銀、市場動揺も、政策運営は政府と市場の「板挟み」が続く ～一部の利上げ観測に「ゼロ回答」、様々な政策目標実_a6041851.pdf
Report source: DLRI
Date: 2026-10-21
Pages: 3
Extraction status: ok

### Page 1

Asia Trends

2026 年10 月 21 日

1/3 
インドネシア中銀、市場動揺も、政策運営は政府と市場の「板挟み」が続く 
～一部の利上げ観測に「ゼロ回答」、様々な政策目標実現へ難しい対応が続くと予想される～ 
 経済調査部 主席エコノミスト  西濵 徹 
（℡：050-5474-7495）

（要旨） 
○ インドネシア中銀は 10 月 20～21 日の理事会で、政策金利のＢＩレートを４会合連続で
5.75％に据え置いた。MSCI が市場の透明性やガバナンスへの懸念を背景に 「スタンドアロー
ン市場」への格下げ案を示し、株安・ルピア安が進んだことで、一部では利上げ観測が浮上
していた。しかし、中銀は５～６月の累計100bp の利上げで物価安定には十分との認識を示
し、預金ファシリティ金利を 25bp 引き上げて 5.00％とするとともに、為替ヘッジ取引への
優遇措置を強化し、通貨安定と海外資金の流入促進を図る判断を示した。 
○ 一方で、 景気下支えや雇用創出に向けて、 預金準備率を11～12 月に段階的に引き下げ、 銀行
の貸出余力を拡大する方針を示した。原油高に伴うインフレ圧力や、米国の金融引き締めに
よるドル高圧力を警戒する一方、国内消費の底堅さや民間投資の持ち直し、外需の回復など
を踏まえ、2026 年の経済成長率見通しは「＋4.9～5.7％」との従来の見通しを据え置いた。 
○ 中銀を巡ってはここ数年、政策目標に物価・通貨の安定に加え、経済成長や雇用創出を支援
する役割が追加されてきたほか、政治介入に対する懸念もある。こうしたなか、先行きも政
府の景気下支え要請と金融市場の通貨安定要求との「板挟み」となり、様々な政策目標の実
現を目指す観点から、一段と難しい政策運営を迫られる状況が続くと予想される。 
【インドネシア中銀、市場の混乱で一部に政策金利の引き上げ観測も、４会合連続で据え置き】 
インドネシア銀行（中銀）は、10 月 20～21 日に開催した月例理事会において、政策金利である
ＢＩレートを４会合連続で 5.75％に据え置くことを決定した。一方で、預金ファシリティ金利を
25bp 引き上げ、5.00％とした。中銀は、５月の月例理事会でルピア相場の安定を目的に50bp の大
幅利上げを決定したものの、その後も金融市場ではルピア安の動きが収まらず、株式、債券も下落
するトリプル安に直面した。このため、中銀は６月９日に開催した週次理事会で 25bp の緊急利上
げを実施するとともに、ルピア相場の安定に向けた追加対策を発表した。さらに、６月の月例理事
会でも 25bp の追加利上げを実施し、約１ヵ月の間に累計 100bp もの利上げを行うなど、通貨安定
に向けて金融引き締めを強化した。その後は、ルピア相場が落ち着きを取り戻したことから、前回
の９月会合まで３会合連続で政策金利を据え置き、様子見姿勢を維持してきた。 
しかし、 足元では同国の金融市場が再び混乱に直面している。 きっかけは、 指数算出会社のMSCI

### Page 2

2/3 
が 10 月15 日、同国市場を「スタンドアローン市場」に格下げする案を示し、市場参加者との協議
を開始したことにある（注１）。MSCI は
１月、同国株式市場の情報開示に懸念を
表明し、透明性向上への取り組みが進ま
なければ、フロンティア市場に格下げす
る可能性を示唆した。これを受けて、当
局は最低浮動株比率の大幅引き上げな
ど、海外投資家の懸念解消に向けた対策
を打ち出し、市場改革を加速させる姿勢
を示した。しかし、その後も市場参加者
からは、協調的な取引行為への疑念が解
消されておらず、実効性のある法執行も
確認できないとの指摘が MSCI に寄せられた。また、フロンティア市場への格下げでは、問題の深
刻さが適切に反映されないとの意見も寄せられた。このため、MSCI は 2027 年４月末まで市場参加
者の意見を募り、 同年６月末までに最終判断を示すとした。 協議を通じて市場のガバナンスや機能
の実質的な改善を裏付ける明確な証拠が確認されない限り、 格下げを進める方針である。 この発表
を受けて、主要株価指数（ジャカルタ総合指数）は一段と下振れしており、ルピア相場も下落して
いる（図１） 。 
こうしたなか、金融市場においては、月例理事会を前に、中銀が政策金利の引き上げを含む市場
安定策を打ち出すとの見方が浮上した。また、中銀は今回、会合後の記者会見の開始時刻を前倒し
すると発表した。中銀では、７月にペリー前総裁が任期を２年弱残して突然辞任し、上級副総裁で
あったデストリ・ダマヤンティ氏が総裁代行を務めた後、９月２日に総裁へ正式就任した。今回の
月例理事会は、デストリ氏の正式就任後２回目の定例会合に当たる。しかし、中銀は今回もＢＩレ
ートの引き上げを見送る一方、 預金ファシリティ金利の引き上げや為替ヘッジ取引への優遇措置の
強化を通じて、市場の安定を図る判断を示した。

【中銀は様々な政策目標の実現に向け、政府と市場の間で「板挟み」となることが予想される】 
会合後に公表した声明文では、 今回の決定について 「イラン情勢の緊迫化や米国の金融引き締め
による対外的な圧力が続くなかでのルピア相場の安定」、「2026 年と 2027 年の物価目標の達成」を
目的とする政策ミックスの一環との考えを示した。一方で、 景気下支えと国内の流動性確保に向け
て、 預金準備率の引き下げを決定した。 一般商業銀行向けは現行の９％から、11 月１日に８％、12
月１日に７％へ、 イスラム銀行向けは現行の7.5％から、11 月１日に6.5％、12 月１日に5.5％へ、
それぞれ段階的に引き下げる。さらに、為替ヘッジ取引への優遇措置を強化し、海外からの資金流

注１ 10 月16 日付レポート「インドネシア株に逆風、MSCI が「スタンドアローン」への格下げ検討」 
  （https://www.dlri.co.jp/report/macro/666610.html）

### Page 3

本資料は情報提供を目的として作成されたものであり、投資勧誘を目的としたものではありません。作成時点で、第一ライフ資産運用
経済研究所が信ずるに足ると判断した情報に基づき作成していますが、その正確性、完全性に対する責任は負いません。見通しは予告
なく変更されることがあります。また、記載された内容は、第一生命保険ないしはその関連会社の投資方針と常に整合的であるとは限
りません。 
3/3 
入を促す方針を示した。 
世界経済については「イラン情勢の悪化による原油価格の高止まりを受けて、 景気の下押しとイ
ンフレ圧力が併存している」との認識を示す一方、 「2026 年の世界経済成長率は＋３％となる」と
の従来見通しを維持した。 また、 「米国で追加利上げの可能性が高まっているうえ、 財政政策を理由
に米国債金利は高止まりしている」 として、 「新興国への資金流入が抑制され、 ドル高圧力が続く可
能性がある」との見通しを示した。 
一方、 同国経済について 「財政支援や消費者心理の改善を追い風に家計消費は底堅く推移してい
る」としつつ、足元では「企業マインドの改善を背景に民間投資に持ち直しの兆しがでている」と
の認識を示した。先行きは「外需の増加
も見込まれる」として、 「2026 年の経済
成長率は＋4.9～5.7％になる」との従来
見通しを据え置いた。そして、対外収支
も「世界的な不確実性の高まりの影響を
緩和すべく、国際収支を強化する必要が
ある」 としつつ、 「９月末時点の外貨準備
高は輸入の5.3 ヵ月分を有する」とし、
「2026 年の経常赤字はＧＤＰ比▲2.4～
▲1.6％」になると３会合ぶりに見通し
を示した。ルピア相場については「為替
介入やヘッジ取引への優遇措置などを通じた安定強化を図っている」 と従来からの対策を強化する
考えを示した。 そして、 物価動向については 「政府が設定した目標の範囲内で維持されている」 （図
２） ほか、 足元では 「天候不順による食料インフレの懸念が高まっている」ものの、「目標域内に収
めるべく、為替相場の安定に加え、政府も食料インフレ対策を強化する」との見方を示した。 
会合後に記者会見に臨んだ中銀のデストリ総裁は、MSCI による発表について 「株式市場の動向を
注視している」との見方を示した。そのうえで、政策運営について「的を絞ったものにしたい」と
して、 「海外からの投資の呼び込みに資するものにしたい」との考えを示した。そして、 「我々は５
月以降すでに100bp の利上げを実施しており、 物価安定には十分」 、 「海外投資家に対してインセン
ティブを提供している」との考えを示した。先行きの政策運営については「データ次第になる」と
して明言を避けた。また、ソリキン副総裁は、預金準備率引き下げの狙いについて「雇用創出を目
的としたもの」 、 「銀行の貸し出し余力の拡大を目指したもの」と説明した。そして、今回の政策対
応について「様々な政策目的に対応することを目指したもの」と説明した。 
こうした発言の背景には、ここ数年、中銀に対して、物価と通貨の安定に加え、経済成長や雇用
創出を支援する役割も求められるようになっていることがあると考えられる。 政治介入が懸念され
る動きもみられるなか、中銀の政策運営はこれまで以上に難しくなっている。先行きも、中銀は政
府からの景気下支え要請と、金融市場からの通貨安定要求との「板挟み」に置かれるとともに、難
しい政策運営を迫られる局面が続くと予想される。                  以 上

## Document 491: コロンビア、右派政権誕生で対米関係改善、ペソ相場に持ち直しの動き ～原油高と高金利が支え、先行きは政策実現力とインフレ対応が焦点～

Source PDF: all report pdf/コロンビア、右派政権誕生で対米関係改善、ペソ相場に持ち直しの動き ～原油高と高金利が支え、先行きは政策実現力とインフレ対応が焦点～.pdf
Report source: DLRI
Date: 2026-10-20
Pages: 3
Extraction status: ok

### Page 1

World Trends

2026 年10 月 20 日

1/3 
コロンビア、右派政権誕生で対米関係改善、ペソ相場に持ち直しの動き 
～原油高と高金利が支え、先行きは政策実現力とインフレ対応が焦点～ 
 経済調査部 主席エコノミスト  西濵 徹 
（℡：050-5474-7495）

（要旨） 
○ コロンビアでは、６月の大統領選挙で、治安対策の強化や対米関係改善を掲げたデラエスプ
リエジャ氏が勝利し、中南米で続く右派政権拡大の流れを象徴する結果となった。８月の新
政権発足後は、トランプ米政権による安全保障支援や麻薬対策での連携強化を通じ、前政権
下で悪化した対米関係は急速に改善している。一方、同氏は政治経験を欠くうえ、左翼ゲリ
ラ掃討などの強硬な治安政策、貧困対策、国家機構縮小や減税、エネルギー産業強化など幅
広い政策を掲げるものの、議会で与党が少数派であるため、政策実現のハードルは高い。 
○ 金融市場では政権交代をいったん好感したものの、原油高によるインフレやＦＲＢの利上げ
に伴う米ドル高を受けて、通貨ペソは下落圧力にさらされた。国内でもエネルギー価格上昇
やエルニーニョ現象による農業生産の低迷を背景に、生活必需品を中心にインフレ圧力が強
まっており、中銀は９月末に追加利上げを決定するなどタカ派姿勢を強めている。 
○ 足元では、中銀による高金利維持姿勢を背景に高利回りを求める資金が流入し、ペソ相場は
持ち直している。先行きは引き続き米ドル相場の動向に注意が必要なものの、原油高の長期
化による産油国としての交易条件改善や高金利を背景とする資金流入が期待される ことか
ら、ペソ相場が比較的堅調に推移する可能性も考えられる。

南米コロンビアでは、 ６月に実施された大統領選挙の決選投票において、無所属ながら極右政党
（国民救済運動）の支援を受けた弁護士のデラエスプリエジャ氏が勝利した （注１） 。中南米では
2020 年代前半、左派政権の誕生が広がる「ピンクの潮流」の動きがみられ、同国でも2022 年に初
の左派政権 （ペトロ政権） が誕生した。 しかし、 ここ数年はアルゼンチン、 コスタリカ、 ボリビア、
エクアドル、 チリ、 そしてペルーで右派政権がドミノ的に誕生するなどピンクの潮流は後退してい
る。背景には、コロナ禍を経た景気低迷に加え、治安悪化も重なる形で有権者の関心が変化してい
ることがある。デラエスプリエジャ氏は当初、泡沫候補とみられたものの、犯罪取り締まりの強化

注１ ６月22 日付レポート「コロンビア大統領選､強硬右派の勝利で親米路線に回帰」 
  （https://www.dlri.co.jp/report/macro/623334.html）

### Page 2

2/3 
を掲げて最終盤にかけて支持を広げた。また、ペトロ前大統領の下では、トランプ米大統領との対
立を理由に対米関係が悪化したが、同氏はトランプ氏の支持を公言して対米関係の改善を目指す姿
勢をみせたことも支持を後押しした。 
８月に新政権が発足すると、トランプ米政権は同国に対する 10 億ドル規模の安全保障支援を行
うことを明らかにしたほか、 麻薬対策での連携強化を確認するなど、 対米関係は急速に改善に向か
っている。一方で、デラエスプリエジャ氏は政治経験がなく、その手腕は未知数である。大統領選
では、政治経験のなさを逆手に取り、アウトサイダーとして政治変革を実現すると主張したほか、
派手なパフォーマンスや過激な言動を通じて、自らを力強さの象徴である「虎」になぞらえる選挙
運動を展開した。さらに、公約として、爆撃による左翼ゲリラの掃討のほか、コカインの原料とな
るコカの栽培地への除草剤の散布など強硬路線への転換を図る考えを示した。そのうえで、 大規模
刑務所の建設のほか、教育・医療・住宅の改善を通じた貧困削減を目指すとした。一方で、前政権
の下で財政状況が急速に悪化していることを受けて、 国家機構の規模を最大４割縮小して減税を行
うほか、 輸出の３割を占める原油や石油製品、 天然ガス関連産業を強化する考えをみせた。 しかし、
共和国議会において、同政権を支える与党は上下院ともに少数派にとどまるため、政策実現のハー
ドルは極めて高い。 
このため、金融市場ではデラエスプリエジャ氏の勝利をいったん好感したものの、その後は様子
見姿勢に転じる動きがみられた。さら
に、 イラン情勢の悪化を受けた原油高が
世界的なインフレを招くなか、ＦＲＢ
（米連邦準備制度理事会） の利上げ実施
などを受けた米ドル高も重なり、 同国の
通貨ペソ相場は調整の動きを強めた。 同
国は産油国ではあるものの、 原油高によ

れないうえ、 足元ではエルニーニョ現象
による農業生産の低迷が食料インフレ
を招くなど、 生活必需品を中心とするイ

こうしたなか、中銀は９月末に開催した定例会合において追加利上げを決定した。会合では、７
人の理事のうち４人が25bp、１人は 50bp の利上げを主張していたことが明らかになった。その後
も、 理事の間から政策金利を長期間にわたって高水準に維持する必要があるとの主張が示されるな
ど、中銀がタカ派姿勢を強めていることが確認されている。 
足元ではＦＲＢによる追加利上げ観測が残るものの、 中銀が高金利政策を志向していることを受
けて、ペソ相場は持ち直している（図２） 。先行きも米ドル相場の動向を注視する必要はあるもの
の、イラン情勢を巡る不透明感が続き、原油高の長期化も予想されるなか、産油国である同国の交

⾦融管理部⻔ 11 ⽉ 2 ⽇将召开会议，研究推动⽀持资本市场⼯具使⽤
上证报记者从业内获悉，⾦融管理部⻔ 11 ⽉ 2 ⽇将召开会议。有证券公司、基⾦公司接到通知，
11 ⽉ 2 ⽇中国⼈⺠银⾏、中国证监会召开会议，研究推动⽀持资本市场⼯具使⽤。同⼀天，中国
⼈⺠银⾏、⾦融监管总局、中国证监会还将召集各⼤银⾏开会研究优化股票回购增持再贷款政

股票回购增持再贷款是⽀持资本市场的两项货币政策⼯具之⼀。 2024 年 10 ⽉ 18 ⽇，中国⼈⺠银
⾏联合⾦融监管总局、中国证监会发布《关于设⽴股票回购增持再贷款有关事宜的通知》，设⽴
股票回购增持再贷款，激励引导⾦融机构向符合条件的上市公司和主要股东提供贷款，⽀持其
回购和增持上市公司股票。

选）。共和党众议院 218 席对 214 席、参议院 53 席对 47 席，⺠主党须在众议院净增 3 席、参议院净
增 4 席翻盘；若打成 50:50 由副总统万斯投关键⼀票。拨款、税法案与⼈事确认权的归属由此改
写财政、关税与产业补贴的推进难度。博彩市场⽬前将⺠主党 “ 横扫 ” 两院的概率定价在 67% 。

Marvell 洽谈收购 Lumentum （最快 11 ⽉ 2 ⽇当周宣布）

交易最快可能于下周宣布。不过双⽅尚未达成最终协议，包括时间安排在内的细节仍有变数。
苹果： Apple 智能接⼊阿⾥千问， 11 ⽉ 4 ⽇开放测试
10 ⽉ 31 ⽇，苹果和阿⾥巴巴宣布达成合作，将千问集成⾄ Apple 智能，中国⽤⼾可以在 Siri 、写
作⼯具及视觉智能中，直接调⽤千问的⽂本与图像理解、内容⽣成等能⼒。这些功能将于 11 ⽉ 4
⽇起随最新的 iOS 开发者 Beta 版开放测试，并将于 12 ⽉ iOS 27.3 正式发布时⾯向中国⽤⼾推出。
上海进博会  + 虹桥论坛（ 11 ⽉ 5 ⽇ -10 ⽇）

级开放平台，进博会每年集中释放中国进⼝订单与准⼊信号；成交意向⾦额及消费品、⾼端装
备、医疗展区签约情况是看点，牵动进⼝链与⼈⺠币资产情绪。

—— 携⼿构建⽹络空间命运共同体 " ，设 27 个分论坛含 AI 乌镇对话、机器⼈与具⾝智能。据中国
政府⽹，⼤会邀请全球政府、国际组织、企业与专家学者代表，围绕⼈⼯智能开源开放发展的
理念与实践展开交流。
上交所科创芯⽚ ETF 出海，将在⾹港联交所上市
上交所科创芯⽚ ETF （ 588200 ）将通过沪港 ETF 互挂实现出海。跟踪该 ETF 的港交所互挂基⾦
已于 10 ⽉ 28 ⽇获批，预计 11 ⽉ 6 ⽇在⾹港联交所上市，境外投资者可在港股市场直接交易，便捷
配置上证科创板芯⽚指数资产。
AI ⼤模型追踪： Kimi K3.1 Pro 完整模型权重开放、 Claude Fable 5.5 或发布

10 ⽉ 24 ⽇凌晨，⽉之暗⾯发布 Kimi K3.1 系列⾸个模型 Kimi K3.1 Pro 及⾼速版 Kimi K3.1 Pro
Highspeed ，完整模型权重计划于 11 ⽉ 4 ⽇前开放。第三⽅倒计时站预估 Claude Fable 5.5 11 ⽉
5 ⽇发布， Anthropic 官⽅未公布⽇期与模型 ID ；微软 Azure East US 2 ⽬录已出现 claude-fable-
5-5 条⽬，标注 1M 上下⽂、最⼤输出 12.8 万 token 、输⼊每百万 10 美元，时点紧贴 Anthropic 11

## Document 541: 債券市場總評：非投等高息有撐

Source PDF: all report pdf/債券市場總評：非投等高息有撐.pdf
Report source: Fubon
Date: 2026-10-05
Pages: 7
Extraction status: ok

### Page 1

1

台北富邦銀行 
Oct 5，2026 | 3:00 PM (GMT+8) 
債券市場總評： 非投等高息有撐
投資研究部 
 Research 
Financial Market 
▎美國經濟具韌性，支撐年底升息預期 
9 月下旬以來，美債殖利率快速上行，主要因經濟數
據仍具韌性，加上中東情勢反覆推升油價與通膨疑
慮，牽動市場持續評估後續升息空間。雖然 8 月 PCE
與核心 PCE 低於預期，但先前公布的 9 月製造業與服
務業 PMI 大幅優於預期，第 2 季 GDP 終值上修，顯
示經濟動能仍強。但最新 9 月非農意外疲弱，新增就
業僅 2.9 萬人、失業率升至 4.2%，降低短期升息急迫
性，10 月升息機率降溫，但 12 月升息預期仍存，市
場轉向觀望 9 月 CPI 公布，10 年期美債殖利率短暫回
落後仍處高檔。 
圖 1 就業數據降溫，惟美債殖利率仍處高檔

資料來源：Bloomberg，2026/10/5 
▎殖利率攀升至高位，債市同步下滑 
近日部分 Fed 官員表示，後續利率決策仍需觀察更多
數據，略為緩和市場對進一步升息的定價，帶動 10 年
期美債殖利率自高點小幅回落至 5.25%。不過，殖利
率與油價仍處高檔，限制債市修復空間。9 月下旬以
來，主要債券 YTD 報酬同步下滑，美國公債與投資級
債跌幅較深，非投等債則因存續期較短、風險偏好尚
穩，跌幅相對較輕。

圖 2 利率與油價高檔，主要債券報酬下滑

資料來源：Bloomberg，2026/10/5 
◼ 近期美國數據仍具韌性 ， 中東局勢推升油價與通膨疑慮 ，PCE 雖低於預期 ，但 PMI
與 GDP 仍強，非農轉弱緩和升息預期，美債殖利率仍處高檔，債市承壓。美非投
等債受高息收與中短存續期緩衝 ， 跌幅較輕 ， 殖利率升至8.3%， 處近10 年高位。 
◼ 美非投等債信用結構較過去改善，較高評級的 BB 級占比已逾半，違約率約 2%，
位於近 25 年低檔。美非投等企業 2Q 營收與 EBITDA 分別年增 10.4%、14.4%，
增速加快並創近 4 年較強表現 ， 獲利增速快於營收 ， 也帶動槓桿與償息指標改善。 
◼ 殖利率高檔震盪下，債券報酬較難仰賴價格貢獻，息收成為更重要的收益來源。
美非投等債因票息較高、存續期較短，可提供一定報酬緩衝。後續可依風險承受
度，關注科技、金屬與礦業、能源等基本面改善較明顯產業，並偏向 BB、短中天
期及現金流較穩定發行公司。

2026 年初至今報酬率 
美國非投等債報酬率 0.14% 
美國投等債報酬率 -2.74% 
美國公債報酬率 -3.13%

美國 10 年公債殖利率 5.25% 
西德州原油期貨 $90/桶 
美國 10 年期公債殖利率 5.25% 
美國基準利率 3.75~4% 
9 月非農就業新增 2.9 萬人 
9 月失業率 4.2% 
8 月 PCE 年增率 3.4% 
8 月核心 PCE 年增率 3%

2

誠信親切、專業創新 週報 
▎高息收提供緩衝，支撐非投等債韌性 
近期公債殖利率快速上行 ， 推升企業債殖利率至近年相
對高位，其中美國非投資等級債到期殖利率已來到約
8.3%，顯著高於過去 10 年平均，殖利率水準已具備相
當吸引力。 
圖 3 美非投等債殖利率攀升至 8.3%，10 年相對高檔

資料來源：Bloomberg，2026/10/1

觀察近 6 個月各類債券報酬來源 ， 今年美國公債殖利率
攀升，使債券報酬較難仰賴價格貢獻，息收重新成為影
響總報酬的關鍵來源。其中，美國非投資等級債因具備
較高殖利率與中短存續期，且息收水準相對較高，較能
緩衝利率上行對價格的影響， 因此相較公債與投等債，
回落幅度相對有限。

圖 4 利息收益貢獻為債券報酬主要來源

資料來源：JPM，2026/9/30

▎美經濟相對穩健 ， 非投等BB 比重提升 
從已開發國家 GDP 年增率來看，美國、歐元區與日本
未來幾季仍維持正成長， 其中美國成長表現相對穩健 ，
預估仍維持約 2%左右增速 ， 顯示高利率與油價波動下 ，
經濟仍具一定韌性。

圖 5 美國經濟動能仍相對歐元區、日本較具韌性

資料來源：JPM，2026/9/26

信用結構方面， 美國非投等債市場債信結構已較金融海
嘯前改善，BB 評級占比由 2007 年 12 月的 32%升至
2026 年 5 月的 54%，目前占整體非投等債比重過半，
CCC 與未評級占比則由 15%降至 7%。 由於BB 是非投
等債中評級最高的一層，信用風險低於 B 與 CCC，BB
級占比提高代表目前美國非投等債市場中 ，信用體質較
接近投資等級的發行公司占比明顯提升。 
圖 6 美國非投等債各信用評級占比與違約率

資料來源：JPM，2026/8/31 
% 
過去 6 個月，債券報酬組成因子 
% 
美國非投等債殖利率 8.35% 
美國投等債殖利率 6.01%

10 年平均 3.97%

10 年平均 6.62% 
目前違約率 依信用評級之指數權重

### Page 3

3

誠信親切、專業創新 週報 
違約率方面，目前美國非投等債整體違約率約 2%，落
在歷史區間相對低位 ， 低評級債違約壓力也未見明顯擴
散。在美國經濟仍具韌性、非投等債 BB 比重提升且違
約率受控的背景下 ， 美國非投等債的高殖利率仍具基本
面支撐。

▎美非投等企業，2Q 營收獲利顯著改善 
檢視美國非投等債企業第 2 季基本面，營收與 EBITDA
延續正成長動能，且2Q26 成長幅度進一步加速。美國
非投等債企業營收季增 9.2%、 年增10.4%，EBITDA 季
增 19.2%、 年增14.4%， 營收與EBITDA 年增率雙雙創
下 4 年來最高，顯示企業營運表現仍具韌性。

圖 7  美非投等企業營收年增 10.4%，4 年來最快增速

資料來源：JPM，2026/9/15

從產業表現來看，今年商品、能源與企業投資需求仍延
續，金屬與礦業、能源、科技、零售與服務業等產業對
整體營收與 EBITDA 成長形成貢獻，使 2Q 基本面改善
具備一定廣度。EBITDA 增速高於營收增速，代表企業
獲利擴張速度快於收入成長，反映成本控管、營運效率
或價格轉嫁能力仍在。 
圖 8 EBITDA 年增顯著提升至 14%，4 年來較強增速

資料來源：JPM，2026/9/15 
▎美非投等槓桿回落，償息能力改善 
2Q 美國非投等債企業信用指標出現改善，槓桿率降至 
4.51 倍 ， 主要反映EBITDA 成長速度快於債務增加 ， 帶
動槓桿指標修復。

圖 9 美非投等企業槓桿率回落至 4.51 倍

資料來源：JPM，2026/9/15

償息能力方面，儘管利息費用仍在增加，但 EBITDA 成
長仍帶動利息保障倍數回升至 4.12 倍，2 年較高水準 ，
顯示企業獲利對利息支出的覆蓋能力有所改善 ， 高利率
環境下，美非投等企業的償息能力仍相對有撐。整體來
看，2Q26 美國非投等債企業營收、獲利與信用指標同
步改善，基本面韌性仍在。

圖 10 美非投等企業利息保障倍數升至 2 年高位

資料來源：JPM，2026/9/15

### Page 4

4

誠信親切、專業創新 週報 
▎非投等具高息收優勢 ， 關注BB 短天期 
近期公債殖利率仍在高檔波動 ， 美非投等債短線表現受
到壓抑。不過，經過利率重新定價後，美非投等債殖利
率已回到相對具吸引力的水準 ， 較高息收與相對較短存
續期，仍可在利率波動環境下提供一定報酬緩衝。 
綜合前述，美國經濟相對歐日仍較穩健，企業營收、獲
利與信用指標同步改善 ， 為美非投等債後續表現提供基
本面基礎。產業面可留意科技、基礎工業相關的金屬與
礦業、能源等基本面改善較明顯的方向。整體而言，後
續可偏向 BB、短中天期及現金流較穩定的發行公司，
參與較高息收帶來的收益機會。

## Document 616: 地方選で大敗が続くドイツ与党

Source PDF: all report pdf/地方選で大敗が続くドイツ与党.pdf
Report source: MURC
Date: 2026-09-24
Pages: 4
Extraction status: ok

### Page 1

2026 年 9 月 24 日

MURC Focus

地方選で大敗が続くドイツ与党

～ドイツの政局不安がユーロ売り要因となる可能性～

調査部 主任研究員 土田 陽介

1. 地方選での大敗が相次いだ与党 
ドイツの地方選で中道右派の与党・キリスト教民主同盟（CDU）の大敗が相次いでいる。9 月 6 日のザクセン・
アンハルト州に続き、9 月 20 日のベルリン州（特別州）と メクレンブルク・フォアポンメルン州 の州議会選挙で、
CDU は得票率を大幅に減らした。特に メクレンブルク・フォアポンメルン 州議会選挙では、 CDU の得票率が
4.9%にとどまり、5%阻止条項（得票率 5%未満の政党は議席を保有できない決まり）に従い議席を失った。

他方で、右派政党「ドイツのための選択肢」（ AfD）が勝利を重ねている（図表 1）。ザクセン・アンハルト州での
得票率は 43.8%（前回 20.8%）、ベルリン州は 16.3%（同 9.1%）、メクレンブルク・フォアポンメルン州は 38.2%
（同 16.7%）といずれも得票率を上昇させた。ただしベルリン州の場合は、左派の左翼党（Die Linke） が得票率
25.7%（同 12.3%）で第一党となり、AfD の党勢を上回っている。 
図表 1. CDU と AfD の得票率の比較 図表 2. 主要政党の支持率

（注）連邦議会は 2025 年総選挙での得票率。 
（出所） 各種資料

（注）FDP は自民党の略称。残りの政党の略称は本文中に明記した。 
（出所）INSA

（%）
〇ドイツでは中道政党への支持が後退する一方、右派と左派への支持が上昇し、政治がいわば“二極化”の様
相を呈している。 その大きな要因の一つに、エネルギー価格の高騰があると考えられる。 
〇2027年に予定されている旧西ドイツの5州での州議会選挙でも右派政党 AfD の勢力が伸長すれば、政権を
担う Union の右派回帰に伴う大連立の崩壊の可能性も高まる。 
〇2027年には4月と5月にフランスの大統領選挙も予定されている。フランスとドイツというEU の両大国での政
局が不安定となれば、ユーロ売りが進むだろう。

### Page 2

有権者の主要政党支持率調査（INSA）を確認しても、CDU と姉妹政党のキリスト教社会同盟（CSU）で構成さ
れる同盟（Union）の支持率低下と AfD の支持率上昇が顕著である（図表 2）。対して、Union と大連立を組む社
民党（SPD）の支持率も低迷している。代わって、環境政党である同盟 90／緑の党（B90/Gr）や、左派党と立場
が近いザーラ・ワーゲンクネヒト同盟（BSW）の支持率は上昇している。

こうしたことから、ドイツでは 中道政党への支持が後退 する一方、右派と左派への支持が上昇し 、政治がいわ
ば“二極化”の様相を呈していることが読み取れる。なお AfD に関しては、いずれの政党も現状では連立協議を
拒絶している状況である。ただしメクレンブルク・フォアポンメルン州の場合、AfD が今回の州選挙で 32 議席（定
数 71 議席）を獲得しているため、他党との協議次第では AfD が政権を獲得できる可能性がある。

2. エネルギー価格の急騰が与党離れに 
CDU や SPD の人気凋落の大きな要因の一つに、エネルギー価格の高騰があると考えられる（図表3）。ドイツ
のエネルギー価格は今年 2 月に生じたイラン発のエネルギーショックを受けて急騰。その後は停戦交渉の開始を
受けて落ち着くかに見えたが、結局は停戦交渉が決裂したため反発した。特にガソリンなど化石燃料の価格上昇
は著しく、ドイツ国民の生活を直撃している。

こうした環境の下で問われているのが、かつてドイツに安価なエネルギーを供給していたロシアとの距離感であ
る。AfD はロシアと近しく、支持者の中にもロシアとの関係改善を図るべきだという機運が強い。一方、AfD 以外
の政党の支持者は、引き続きロシアを脅威とみなす傾向が強い（図表4）。実際、AfD 以外の主要政党はロシアと
の関係改善には基本的に慎重な姿勢である。

図表 3. ドイツのエネルギー価格（消費者物価） 図表 4. 対ロシア意識調査（9 月 3 日時点）

（注）各種燃料、電気料金等が含まれる。 
（出所）EU 統計局（ユーロスタット）

（出所）ARD-Deutschland survey

### Page 3

それどころか、フリードリヒ・メルツ首相が率いる中道の大連立政権（Union と SPD）は、ロシアの脅威を念頭に
防衛体制の強化に努めている。その反面でメルツ政権は、CDU 所属のカテリーナ・ライヒェ連邦経済・エネルギ
ー大臣と SPD のカールステン・シュナイダー環境大臣の対立もあり、エネルギー供給安定化のための有効な手
立てを打てていない。こうした不手際が足元の与党離れにつながっていると考えられる。

不法移民対策への不満も CDU や SPD の人気凋落につながっていると指摘される。とはいえ、メルツ政権は
隣接する 9 カ国との間での国境検査の厳格化といった水際対策のみならず、SPD が 2024 年 6 月に導入した
「ターボ帰化」制度（特定技能を持つ一部の移民に最短 3 年で帰化を容認する制度）を廃止するなど、不法移民
対策を推し進めているため、直近の CDU や SPD の人気凋落の主因とは言えないだろう。

3. 政局不安定を嫌気し始めたユーロ相場 
ドイツの次期の総選挙は 2029 年 2 月までに行われるため、州議会選挙の結果が直ちにドイツ国政を揺るがす
ことはない。一方、ドイツでは、2027 年に AfD の勢力がまだ限定的な旧西ドイツの 5 州で州議会選挙が予定さ
れている（図表 5）。これらの州選挙でも AfD の勢力伸長が確認されれば、政権を担う Union の右派回帰に伴う
大連立の崩壊などの可能性も高まる。

こうした政局の不安定さが、底堅かったユーロ相場の変調をもたらす可能性がある。 ベルリン州とメクレンブル
ク・フォアポンメルン州の州議会選挙の翌日である 9 月 21 日の為替市場では、ユーロの対ドル相場が終値で 1
ユーロ＝1.1464 ドルと、前日比 0.17%安となった。欧州中銀（ECB）による利上げ観測などからユーロ相場は底
堅く、9 月 20 日の州議会選挙の結果の影響は限定的だったとみられる。

図表 5. 2027 年に予定されている州議会選挙 図表 6. ユーロ相場

### Page 4

一方、イラン情勢の緊迫化に伴う“有事のドル買い”が再開したこともあり、ユーロ相場の堅調さは徐々に弱まっ
ている。2027 年 4 月 18 日と 5 月 2 日にはフランスの国政選挙（大統領選挙）が予定されており、波乱が予想さ
れている。こうした中で、旧西ドイツ諸州の州議会選挙でも CDU や SPD の退潮が明らかとなれば、フランスとド
イツという欧州連合（EU）の両大国の政局が不安定となり、ユーロ売りが進むだろう。
